# Decision data of a Windows 10 text/font stack

Generated by `tools/gen_d12.py` from the committed Windows 10 22H2 recordings in `data/win10/` (nothing here is typed by hand or
re-recorded; rerun `python3 tools/gen_d12.py` to regenerate, `--check` to verify). Oracle: Windows 10 Home 22H2, 10.0.19045 (UBR 2965),
en-US (ACP 1252), 1024x768x32, std VGA, one monitor, administrator session, 96 DPI system DPI; the facts of each recording family
(boots, determinism, documented exceptions) are in `data/win10/<family>/README.md`. The recordings are deterministic over two fresh boots
except the cells listed under "Undefined cells and volatile data".

## What this is for

An implementer of a Windows-10-like text and UI stack (font mapper, text metrics, string measurement, dialog and control layout)
needs the *decisions* Windows makes, not only the API contract: which face a LOGFONT realises, what metrics that font has at a
size, how wide each character is, and how big the UI is at each DPI. These tables are those decisions, measured. `tools/gen_golden.py`
turns a sample of the rows into conformance tests (`tests/golden_*`) that pass on a real Windows 10 guest by construction.

Section A answers "requested face / charset / pitch / family -> realised face (and which text falls back)", section B "font x size ->
ascent, descent, leadings, widths, height", section C "face x size x character -> advance width", section D "DPI x manifest -> system
metrics, dialog units, control heights".

## Conventions

- `src` (last column of every table) = `<recording file relative to data/win10> <table>:<row key>`, the key as written in the recording
  (tokens percent-escaped: `%20` space, `~` empty string). Looking the row up: `python3 tools/rec.py show data/win10/<file> --table <table>`.
- A `@` in a key stands for the field(s) listed in the row (a set or a column): the row then stands for one recording row per member.
  `<column file>` / `<column ctx>` in a `src` mean the file/context named by the column header.
- `n/a` = the recording has `!` (call failed or value not available). Heights: `req_h` < 0 is the character (em) height in pixels,
  > 0 the cell height, 0 the default. All lengths are device pixels at the stated DPI. `tmPitchAndFamily`/`tmCharSet` are the
  TEXTMETRIC fields (hex pitch/family byte: 0x01 variable-pitch/fixed bit, 0x02 vector, 0x04 TrueType, 0x08 device; high nibble = family).
- Row order is the order of the recording. Row counts are stated per table.

## Undefined cells and volatile data

Documented exceptions in the recording READMEs, which these tables inherit:

- `GetSystemMetrics` index 93 (`i93`) is volatile between boots (564 vs 588); excluded from D1.
- `GetSystemMetricsForDpi` at 72 DPI: a later process on the same image returned CXMIN 106 where the recording has 128, so the dpi 72
  column of D1 is unverified (not asserted by the golden tests).
- `SystemParametersInfoForDpi` at 72 DPI returns TRUE with an (almost) zero struct and a varying menu height: undefined; B4/B5 do not list 72.
- The DPI sweep and the cold-boot value at 144 differ for the unaware desktop DPI state (ui_metrics README): the `dpi144/168/192`
  columns of D3/D4 come from the sweep recordings and are reference for the probe's unaware process only.
- control_heights at 144/168/192 are the virtualised 96 DPI view of an unaware exe: they equal dpi96 apart from a few system rows. Real
  per-monitor-aware recordings do not exist yet (STATE.md, not done).
- Everything is at 96 system DPI for fonts (B1..B3, C1..C3, A1..A8): font recordings were made at 96 DPI only.
- `!` cells: raster faces have no outline/OTM fields; comctl32 5.82 (no manifest) has no BCM_GETIDEALSIZE/BCM_GETTEXTMARGIN/TTM_GETBUBBLESIZE.
- Observed-only facts: the face substitution rules (A4/A5) and the glyph coverage (A8) are observations of this machine's font set
  (the standard Windows 10 inventory, `font_inventory` recording); on a machine with other fonts installed the realised files differ.

Row counts: A1 215, A2 43, A3 139, A4 205, A5 39, A6 32, A7 67, A8 17, B1 1491, B2 252, B3 14, B4 42, B5 6, C1 150, C2 45, C3 60, D1 44, D2 32, D3 24, D4 176 (total 3093).


## A. Face substitution and fallback


### A1. Requested face -> realised face (the mapper decision)

**For:** What GDI realises when a LOGFONT names a face: the name GetTextFace reports, the family and file that really back the font, the type (tt/raster/vector) and the metrics of the realised font. Covers installed faces, aliases (MS Shell Dlg, Helv, Tms Rmn, Times, Helvetica), vertical `@` faces, case variants, non-ASCII names and ~45 absent names. A face that does not exist is realised as Arial.

**Source:** face_substitution/dpi96.rec.gz `map`, rows `src=base;api=W;req_h=-11;req_cs=1;req_pf=0x0;dc=screen;gm=1` (lfHeight -11, DEFAULT_CHARSET, weight 400, no other fields). 215 rows.

**Undefined cells / limits:** A raster/vector face has no OTM family: the cell shows n/a and the family is the GetTextFace name. `realised file` is the GetFontRealizationInfo file (`.fon` for raster faces). Rows with `req_h=-12` and the api variants (A, ExW, CreateFontW) exist in the recording; this table lists the W api at -11 (see the agreement note below).

**Note:** Api agreement (computed from the same file, `src=base` rows): 215 (face, req_h) groups carry a W row and at least one of A / ExW / CreateFontW; the realised face, family, file, type and TEXTMETRIC (h a d il ave max pf cs) are identical within 215 of them.

### A4. Requested charset -> realised face

**For:** How lfCharSet changes the realised face: a face that lacks the charset is replaced by a face that has it (font linking by charset) and the tmCharSet that comes back. Each row groups the charsets of one face that realise the same family and file.

**Source:** face_substitution/dpi96.rec.gz `map`, rows `src=charset` (api W, lfHeight -11); one row stands for all `req_cs` members listed (key field `req_cs=@`). 205 rows.

**Undefined cells / limits:** Charsets not listed in the recording (only 22 values were requested) are undefined.

| requested face | realised family (OTM or GetTextFace for raster) | realised file | tmPitchAndFamily | req charset -> tmCharSet | src |

### A6. Registry substitution and mapper defaults

**For:** The tables the mapper reads: FontSubstitutes (name -> name[,charset]) and FontMapper\FamilyDefaults (family class -> face used when the requested face is absent).

**Source:** face_substitution/dpi96.rec.gz `reg` (rows for HKLM FontSubstitutes and FontMapper\FamilyDefaults). 32 rows.

**Undefined cells / limits:** The recording carries the 64-bit view and a `(wow32)` copy of FontSubstitutes; only the 64-bit view is listed (the copies are equal, see the `(wow32)` rows).

### A7. Font linking chain (SystemLink)

**For:** The per-face fallback chain Windows consults for glyphs missing from a face: ordered list of `file,face[,scaling]` entries.

**Source:** face_substitution/dpi96.rec.gz `reg`, rows under `FontLink\SystemLink`; one row stands for all ordinals `i` of one base face (key field `i=@`). 67 rows.

**Undefined cells / limits:** Faces without an entry have no chain recorded.

### A8. Glyph coverage per face and script (what falls back)

**For:** For a sample string per script, how many characters have no glyph in the requested face (`GetGlyphIndicesW` with GGI_MARK_NONEXISTING_GLYPHS): 0 = fully covered, otherwise those characters need fallback. Cell = `missing/length`.

**Source:** face_substitution/dpi96.rec.gz `fallback`, rows `q=nonaa;req_h=-12` (one row per face; column = key field `text=@`). 17 rows.

**Undefined cells / limits:** The sample strings are in the recording (`str`/`fallback` tables of the probe); coverage of other characters is undefined. Which face is used for the missing characters is not in this table (see the A7 chain).

## B. Font metrics


### B1. TEXTMETRIC per face and size, regular weight

**For:** Ascent, descent, internal leading, average and maximum character width and cell height of every face at every requested height (character heights -6..-40, default 0, cell heights 6..40), as GetTextMetrics reports them on a 96-DPI screen DC (MM_TEXT, GM_COMPATIBLE). Negative `req_h` = em height in pixels, positive = cell height.

**Source:** font_metrics/dpi96.rec.gz `tm`, rows `wt=400;it=0;q=default;cs=1;gm=compat` (DEFAULT_CHARSET, the main grid). 1491 rows.

**Undefined cells / limits:** 96 DPI only (no DPI sweep of this family; the pixel heights of an unaware process do not depend on the system DPI, README of font_metrics). Other DPIs: see B4 (UI fonts) and the sweeps in section D. Values for the empty face and the absent face are the realised fallback (Arial), not a separate font.

### B4. System UI fonts per DPI

**For:** The LOGFONT of each system UI font (SPI_GETNONCLIENTMETRICS / SPI_GETICONMETRICS / icon title) for each DPI, with the metrics of the font realised from it. `dpi=sys` is the process view (no ForDpi call), the others are SystemParametersInfoForDpi(dpi).

**Source:** font_metrics/dpi96.rec.gz `ui_lf` + `ui_tm` (same keys `src;dpi`). 42 rows.

**Undefined cells / limits:** `dpi=72` is not recorded for these tables (SystemParametersInfoForDpi at 72 DPI returns TRUE with garbage, README of dpi_scaling: undefined cell).

## C. Character widths


### C1. Character-width classes, ASCII 0x20..0x7F

**For:** The advance width in device pixels of every printable ASCII character (plus DEL, 0x7F), grouped into width classes per face and height: a layout engine that has these classes knows every ASCII advance. Widths are GetCharWidth32W values (integer pixels, hinted, 96 DPI screen DC).

**Source:** char_widths/dpi96.rec.gz `cw32` (GetCharWidth32W per character), rows `wt=400;it=0`, blocks asc2, asc3, asc4, asc5, asc6, asc7; one row stands for those blocks (key field `blk=@`). 150 rows.

**Undefined cells / limits:** Only regular weight, italic off, heights as listed; the recording also has bold, italic and other heights (cw32 table). Kerning is not applied (see the `kern` table).

### C2. Character-width classes, Latin-1 0xA0..0xFF

**For:** As C1 for the Latin-1 supplement, at the three heights that are the core UI sizes.

**Source:** char_widths/dpi96.rec.gz `cw32` (GetCharWidth32W per character), rows `wt=400;it=0`, blocks lat1a, lat1b, lat1c, lat1d, lat1e, lat1f; one row stands for those blocks (key field `blk=@`). 45 rows.

**Undefined cells / limits:** A character absent from a face is measured as the default glyph width (the recording does not mark it here; see the `gi` table for glyph indices).

### C3. String extents

**For:** GetTextExtentPoint32W width (cx) of reference strings per face and height: pangram, digits, ten W, ten i, the alphabet, common button/menu captions. These are the sums of the C1 advances; they check an implementation's string measurement end to end. The strings are in the recording's `str` table.

**Source:** char_widths/dpi96.rec.gz `ext`, rows `wt=400;it=0` (one row per face and height; column = key field `s=@`). 60 rows.

**Undefined cells / limits:** Only the listed strings, regular weight. The recording also holds cy, fit counts and per-character extents (`ext`, `exdx`).

## D. UI metrics and control heights


### D1. GetSystemMetricsForDpi

**For:** System metrics (frame, caption, scrollbar, icon, menu, edge sizes) for every DPI; the per-DPI scaling rule of the non-client area in pixels.

**Source:** ui_metrics/dpi96.rec.gz `sm_dpi` (one row per metric; column = key field `dpi=@`). 44 rows.

**Undefined cells / limits:** Index 93 (undocumented) is volatile between boots and is excluded (README of ui_metrics). The recording has the other indices 0..99 and 0x1000..0x2004 too (screen size, work area and similar are session state, not DPI rules). The dpi 72 column is unverified: a later guest run returned a different CXMIN there (106 vs 128), so below 96 DPI treat cells as undefined. At dpi 72 the values are as returned (e.g. CYCAPTION 24 > 96-DPI 23); the 72-DPI SystemParametersInfoForDpi values are undefined, see B4.

### D2. AdjustWindowRectExForDpi

**For:** The non-client margins (left, top, right, bottom, in pixels) that a window style adds around a client area, for each DPI, with and without a menu bar: the recording adjusts a 100x100 client rectangle and stores -left, -top, right-100, bottom-100.

**Source:** ui_metrics/dpi96.rec.gz `awr_dpi` (one row per style/ex/menu; column = key field `dpi=@`). 32 rows.

**Undefined cells / limits:** Only the styles and extended styles listed; the recording has 15 styles x 9 extended styles x 2 menu values.

### D3. Dialog base units and DLU control sizes

**For:** How dialog units turn into pixels (`dbu` = GetDialogBaseUnits) and the pixel size of 23 common controls created from a dialog template (MS Shell Dlg 8 pt, modal frame, DS_SETFONT): the DPI-dependent layout table of a dialog engine.

**Source:** `ui_metrics/<column file>` tables `dbu` and `ctl` (template `tmpl=std;style=setfont;face=MS Shell Dlg;pt=8;wt=0;it=0;frame=modal`); the column header names file and thread DPI context (key field `ctx=`); key field `cid=` is the row. 24 rows.

**Undefined cells / limits:** The probe exe has no DPI manifest. FACT (README of ui_metrics): at 144/168/192 the `proc` context sees virtualised 96 DPI (not listed: it equals dpi96), the `system` context shows the real-DPI sizes; the unaware context reports the 96 layout in every file. The `dbu` row is GetDialogBaseUnits as recorded (8x16 in every column except `proc` at 120); it is not a scaling law. Per-monitor contexts (`pm`, `pm2`) equal `system` in the recording and are not repeated. Only the face MS Shell Dlg 8 pt is tabulated; other dialog fonts (Tahoma, Segoe UI, MS Sans Serif) are in the `ctl` table of the recording. The dpi144+ sweep files carry the desktop-DPI state of the 120 stage (ui_metrics README): use cold144 as the reference for unaware rows there.

### D4. Control heights per font, DPI and manifest

**For:** Pixel sizes of common controls (combo box, list box, tree view, list view, toolbar, status bar, rebar, edit) for 11 dialog fonts, per DPI and for the comctl32 v6 manifest: the control-height table of a UI toolkit.

**Source:** `control_heights/<column file>` (columns: unmanifested exe at dpi96/120/144/168/192, and the v6 exe at 96); row = table + key fields `font=<font>;theme=app;cfg=<cfg>`, value column named in the row. 176 rows.

**Undefined cells / limits:** FACT (README of control_heights): the exe is DPI-unaware, so at dpi144/168/192 it sees the virtualised 96 DPI view and the cells equal dpi96; only dpi120 is a real scaled column. Per-monitor/system-aware variants were not recorded (STATE: not done). A font that could not be created or a control that failed shows n/a.
